# nana-pi requirements

The standing, numbered requirement set for the toolkit itself: the pi extension pack, the knowledge pull, the stage layer, the installer, the desk, the bench harness and the repo test runner. nana-pi ships the requirements-first pattern to every project it scaffolds or adopts; this file is the toolkit living under its own rules.

Rows are EARS form (one `shall` sentence per row from the Ubiquitous, WHEN, WHILE, IF-THEN and WHERE templates) and spec the contract, not the design; a row that is not `retired` carries exactly one `shall` outside a code span (G-013). IDs are stable and never renumbered or reused; a removed requirement keeps its ID as `retired`; a split keeps the original ID for the first clause. Cells contain no `|`.

Statuses: `implemented` (a test in this repo asserts it; cited as `<package or app>/tests/<file>::<test title>`, backticked), `untested` (the code does it, nothing pins it), `planned`, `violated` (the code contradicts a documented rule; the cell says where), `retired`. Evidence in a sibling repo carries that repo as a prefix and is exempt from the local rail.

Trace: `npm test` fails when a status disagrees with the suite. A test declares the rows it evidences with a `// req: R-001` comment directly above its `test(` / `check(` call. An `implemented` row with no marker fails; an `untested`, `planned` or `violated` row that a marker traces fails; a marker naming an unknown id fails; a cited test must exist and carry the marker. The rail also reports the count of rows off EARS form on its own `ears:` line every run and fails naming each off-form row once that count exceeds the declared allowance (G-014/G-015). `EARS_ALLOWANCE` in `scripts/requirements-trace.mjs` is 0 since 2026-10-04 and sealed equal to the measured count (R-757). Sealed tunables: a contract number is defined once in the package's config surface with provenance naming its row and pinned by one test; a retune is a requirement diff first.

ID blocks: R-001 to R-199 and R-700 to R-755 the pi extension pack (`packages/nana-pack`; the second block is the pack's continuation after the first filled) · R-200 to R-249 the knowledge pull · R-250 to R-299 the stage layer · R-300 to R-399 the installer · R-400 to R-499 the desk · R-500 to R-599 the bench · R-600 to R-619 the repo test runner · G-001 onward the general engineering requirements every nana project carries. Extracted 2026-10-02 by three read-only passes from CLAUDE.md, HANDOFF.md, the package READMEs, the design docs and every test file; the extraction notes (stale doc claims, judgement calls, what no test pins) are in `docs/requirements-extraction-2026-10-02.md`. · R-373 to R-375 are unused: an installer step Amendment 1 removed before it landed. Continuation blocks declared up front for the EARS-form split (design-ruling.md, ears-form-2026-10-04, §2): R-756 to R-879 the pack's continuation after R-755 (R-756 and R-757 consumed batch 0; R-858 and R-859 consumed 2026-10-05 audit fixes A/B; R-860 to R-864 consumed 2026-10-05 for the code-map generator the templates ship, section 13c (R-863 and R-864 retired before landing; ids never reused) — rest unused until a later batch) · R-880 to R-909 the knowledge pull's continuation · R-910 to R-919 the runner's continuation · R-920 to R-939 the installer's continuation, after R-399 · R-940 to R-959 the desk's continuation, after R-499 (R-943 and R-944 consumed 2026-10-05) · G-013 to G-022 (the EARS form check and the Part G split) consumed batch 0 · R-620 to R-699 the hardening program's block (2026-10-06, `docs/hardening-plan-2026-10-06.md`), allocated per lane: R-620–R-629 review ledger · R-630–R-639 gate · R-640–R-649 session start · R-650–R-654 installer overflow · R-655–R-659 docs · R-660–R-699 later tranches; the installer also uses its free R-392 to R-399, the runner R-916 to R-919 and the desk R-945 to R-949 (tranche 1 consumed, 2026-10-07: R-620–R-625, R-630–R-647, R-650–R-659, R-392–R-399, R-916, R-945–R-949; R-626–R-629 unused; tranche 2 allocations: R-660–R-669 retire, R-670–R-679 trust, R-680–R-689 instructions, R-690–R-699 trial, R-865–R-872 pack, R-873–R-879 frontier, R-917–R-919 and R-950–R-952 and R-898–R-899 tests)

New behaviour in this repo: requirement diff, then tagged tests, then code.

# Part A. The pi extension pack and the repo test runner

| R-857 | IF a stale lock folder cannot be read THEN the remedy shall not claim pi's own trust check fails. | implemented | added 2026-10-05 (edge-builtin-mcp lane, astra r2 MUST): `packages/nana-pack/tests/objective-golden.test.mjs::T17 stale unreadable EMPTY lock folder: remedy never claims pi's own trust check and /trust both fail`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/objective-golden.test.mjs::T17 stale unreadable NON-empty lock folder: remedy never claims pi's own trust check and /trust both fail`; the exact remedy text is pinned by `packages/nana-pack/tests/objective-golden.test.mjs::T2c ${label}: label is its own paragraph right before "governing:"` over the unreadable fixtures (astra r3: the T17 phrase checks alone stay green when the clause is removed) |
| R-760 | IF the store lock path holds a fresh or future-dated lock folder that is empty or cannot be read THEN the remedy shall never tell the owner to delete or move the lock. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/objective-golden.test.mjs::T17 ${k}: remedy never tells the owner to delete or move the lock`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/objective-golden.test.mjs::T17 astra r1 regression: pi's genuinely acquired lock reads store locked, not obstructed`; the astra r2 follow-up (R-856) replaced this row's third original citation with a stronger, remedy-text-asserting check under its own marker, so it no longer sits here — see R-856. widened 2026-10-05 (astra r1 BLOCK) to also cover an UNREADABLE lock folder — the narrowed 2026-10-04 text covered only a readable empty lock. astra acquired a real proper-lockfile lock under a restrictive umask (mode 333, contents unlistable), which the old code classified as lock path obstructed and move it aside even though it is genuinely held: readdirSync throwing is not evidence of non-emptiness, and pi's own stale-reclaim never reads the folder either, only stats and conditionally rmdirs it, so a fresh or future-dated unreadable folder is exactly as held as a readable one. A stale unreadable folder stays obstructed, since we cannot prove it is the empty folder pi's rule would reclaim. Narrowed 2026-10-04 from obstructed or fresh or future-dated, a clause carried over from R-027 that contradicted the documented contract: an obstructed lock path, a file, a link or a non-empty folder, is never a running pi's lock and pi never clears it, so its remedy names it and says to move it aside first, which is R-026 |

## 4. Post-edit checks and receipts

| ID | Requirement | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-084 | WHEN an edit or write succeeds, the pack shall run every postEdit.commands entry whose match regex hits the edited path. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/post-edit-status.test.mjs::a: status text counts the checks and names the file`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/receipt-binding.test.mjs::a: status checks_passed` |
| R-782 | WHEN no postEdit.commands entry's match regex hits the edited path, no status shall be set. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/post-edit-status.test.mjs::e: a run where no command matched sets no status` |
| R-085 | A check failure shall be appended to the tool result so the model sees it, headless runs included. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/post-edit-status.test.mjs::f: headless failure feedback still returned`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/post-edit-status.test.mjs::b: failure tool-result text unchanged`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/receipt-binding.test.mjs::c: failure feedback still returned (control flow unchanged)` |
| R-086 | A passing check shall stay out of the model's context and be reported by the status chip instead. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/post-edit-status.test.mjs::a: a passing run sets a status (the happy path is visible at all)`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/post-edit-status.test.mjs::a: a passing run raises no check-failure toast` |
| R-243 | WHEN every post-edit check passes, nothing shall be appended to the tool result. | untested | split from R-086 2026-10-02 (clause audit): no test pins this clause |
| R-087 | Each failure shall be one line with output line breaks shown as a glyph, the last 2000 chars kept, and truncation stated. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/display-surfaces.test.mjs::post-edit: a truncated failure says so, and keeps the TAIL`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/display-surfaces.test.mjs::post-edit: output line breaks stay legible as ⏎`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/display-surfaces.test.mjs::post-edit: each failure is bounded` |
| R-088 | {file} shall be shell-quoted and substituted so no substitution token is interpreted and no token leaks into the command. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/post-edit-hardening.test.mjs::e: recorded command contains the shell-quoted filename`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/post-edit-hardening.test.mjs::e: no {file} token leaked into the substituted command` |
| R-089 | The edited path shall be resolved the way pi resolves tool paths. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/post-edit-hardening.test.mjs::d: recorded command carries the cwd-resolved absolute path` |
| R-783 | The edited path's pi-resolved value shall feed the command, the digest and the queue key. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/post-edit-hardening.test.mjs::d: relative path check passed (the checker saw the file)`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/post-edit-hardening.test.mjs::d: receipt input still bound repo-relative`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/post-edit-file-queue.test.mjs::checker is gated while pi holds the file's mutation queue`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/post-edit-file-queue.test.mjs::checker ran strictly after the queue was released` |
| R-090 | Checks shall run inside pi's own file-mutation queue, strictly after the edit tool's lock is released. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/post-edit-file-queue.test.mjs::checker is gated while pi holds the file's mutation queue`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/post-edit-file-queue.test.mjs::checker ran strictly after the queue was released`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/post-edit-file-queue.test.mjs::pi's file-mutation queue is importable` |
| R-091 | IF the queue exists but the lock cannot be taken THEN the checker shall not run. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/post-edit-file-queue.test.mjs::b: unlockable file does not silently run the checker` |
| R-784 | IF the queue exists but the lock cannot be taken THEN the receipt shall record not_run. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/post-edit-file-queue.test.mjs::b: receipt records not_run (never passed)` |
| R-785 | IF the queue exists but the lock cannot be taken THEN the model shall be told it did not run. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/post-edit-file-queue.test.mjs::b: refusal is fed back to the model` |
| R-092 | IF the queue module cannot be resolved THEN the check shall still run unserialized. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/post-edit-hardening.test.mjs::${tag}: ${label} — checker received the real file` |

## 10. pi-review: round cap and stall watchdog

*The nana-pack ID range R-001..R-199 is exhausted above; this area and the two that follow
continue in an unreserved R-2xx block (see Extraction notes). R-919 uses the runner continuation
allocation because the pack has no IDs left; the lane brief permits R-918 or R-919 here.*

G-001 and G-002 govern the tunables in `packages/nana-pack/lib/writing-config.mjs`; cited in that module's header, not duplicated here.

## 13b. The writing rule's delivery to pi (nana-pack)

Amendment 1 (design-ruling.md, 2026-10-04, after astra r1 MUST 1): delivery moved from an
agent-dir `AGENTS.md` link (R-373 to R-375, deleted — never landed, IDs stay unused) to the
seventh pack extension, `extensions/nana-writing.ts`, which appends the rule the way
`nana-objective.ts` appends the objective.

| ID | Requirement | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-751 | The pack shall append the writing rule's text to every session's system prompt under the heading ## Writing for Jake (nana), composing with the objective block without disturbing it. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-injection.test.mjs::inject: the rule text follows the base prompt under its heading (production default)`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-injection.test.mjs::inject: reason "${reason}" injects the rule (disposable fixture)` (startup/new/resume/fork/reload), `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-injection.test.mjs::compose: injector orders always produce canonical nana sections and preserve foreign sections` |
| R-752 | IF the rule path is missing, not a regular file, unreadable, or the bytes actually read are not valid UTF-8, THEN the extension shall inject nothing and journal writing_rule_unavailable with the cause. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-injection.test.mjs::unavailable: ${label} injects nothing and journals the cause` (looped over missing file / a directory in its place / invalid UTF-8), `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-injection.test.mjs::unavailable: ${label} journal entry names the cause`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-injection.test.mjs::MUST 1 (subprocess): a FIFO with no writer is refused instantly, never read, never hangs`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-injection.test.mjs::UTF-8 tail: a split € at the read boundary is accepted, no replacement char`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-injection.test.mjs::UTF-8 tail: a split 😀 at the read boundary is accepted, no replacement char`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-injection.test.mjs::UTF-8 tail: 0xff AT the tail is REFUSED with a cause, not silently dropped`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-injection.test.mjs::UTF-8 tail: an invalid byte well inside the window is refused` |
| R-753 | The injected block shall not exceed WRITING_INJECT_CAP chars, with the truncation announced. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-injection.test.mjs::cap: an oversized rule is cut at the cap and the cut is announced`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-injection.test.mjs::seal: WRITING_INJECT_CAP is 4000` |
| R-754 | The extension shall read the rule at every session_start reason, so a reload picks up an edit. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-injection.test.mjs::inject: reason "${reason}" injects the rule (disposable fixture)` (startup/new/resume/fork/reload), `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-injection.test.mjs::reload: the original rule is injected first`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-injection.test.mjs::reload: an edited rule is injected after session_start reason reload` |
| R-755 | The extension shall never request more than WRITING_INJECT_CAP plus READ_MARGIN bytes from the rule file, regardless of the file's actual size (split from R-753, astra r3 MUST 3). | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-injection.test.mjs::seal: READ_MARGIN is 4`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-injection.test.mjs::byte ceiling: a file at or above the cap never requests more than WRITING_INJECT_CAP + READ_MARGIN bytes`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-injection.test.mjs::byte ceiling: a file smaller than the ceiling requests exactly its own size, never more`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-injection.test.mjs::byte ceiling: corrupt bytes exactly past the ceiling are never read (result is unaffected)`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/writing-injection.test.mjs::MUST 1 (subprocess): a 64 MiB rule file under a 32 MiB heap cap does not crash, exits in time, block is capped` |

A bounded read validates only the bytes it reads. Before decoding it trims a trailing sequence whose own lead byte proves it incomplete, never a byte invalid on its own (astra r3 MUST 1; the four UTF-8 tail tests).

## 13c. The code-map generator (templates)

Not a nana-pack module: the generator both project templates ship
(`templates/typescript/template/scripts/code-map.mjs`, `templates/python/template/scripts/code_map.py`),
which G-009/G-010/G-011 already cover generically. nana-pi exercises the TypeScript one
directly, over its own repo, through the shim at `scripts/code-map.mjs` AND, for R-860's full
breadth, by importing the template module itself (tests may import anything, and `templates/`
is not a mapped root — G-007); the Python one has no shim (nana-pi carries no Python source),
so it is exercised only through a rendered project in
`packages/nana-pack/tests/templates-render.test.mjs`. Added 2026-10-05 (map-test-links lane,
Jake's call: "Fix the map" — `research/karpathy-x-2026-10-04.md` background) after measuring
that the TypeScript generator's dynamic-import detection only matched a bare string literal
argument to `import(...)`, never nana-pi's own tests' `await import(new URL("../lib/x.mjs",
import.meta.url).href)` form — 67 of this repo's 95 test modules linked to no mapped module as
a result, and 56 of 77 non-test modules had no direct test caller the map could show. Two
filters were tried after that fix and BOTH retired, in order: R-863 (astra r1 MUST 1: a
comment/string-aware lexer gating all four patterns) lost real edges in ordinary code
(division misread after a completed template or object literal, a `//` comment ended by CR
or U+2028) while still fabricating edges from valid multi-line strings — astra r2,
`docs/reviews/map-test-links-2026-10-05/astra-r2.md`, 2026-10-05. R-864 (that round's own
landing call: a narrower guard dropping only a `//`-line or `*`-line match) ALSO lost real
edges — a generator method's yielded import, a leading `*` multiplying an awaited import's
property, and a real import whose physical line happened to start with `//` because a
multi-line string or a backslash continuation put it there — astra r3,
`docs/reviews/map-test-links-2026-10-05/astra-r3.md`, 2026-10-05. Both filters failed for the
same reason: a regex over raw text cannot reliably tell code from non-code without becoming a
parser, and a half-parser is worse than none, because it can LOSE a real edge instead of
merely adding a spurious one. The seat's final call (astra r3's landing recommendation,
2026-10-05): subtract filtering entirely. The four patterns match raw source exactly as on
`main`, plus DYNAMIC_URL's whitespace tolerance (R-860). The accepted residual — a spurious
edge from text in a string, a template literal or a comment — is recorded in Open questions,
not hidden behind a heuristic that risks the opposite, worse failure. IDs consumed from the
pack's own continuation block (R-756 to R-879), not a new one — template rows already live
there (R-737, R-738, R-756, R-858, R-859); R-863's and R-864's ids are never reused.

| ID | Requirement | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-860 | WHEN a module names a dynamic import whose argument is new URL(<relative string literal>, import.meta.url) — bare, with .href, or with .pathname, with or without a second argument to import(), whitespace allowed around every dot and paren — the TypeScript code-map generator shall record an import edge for it. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/code-map.test.mjs::a dynamic import via new URL(...).href is a mapped edge (the form most of this repo's tests use)`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/code-map.test.mjs::new URL(...) ${f.name} resolves to an edge` (six combinations of bare/.href/.pathname x no-second-argument/a-second-argument, each a distinct target — astra r1 SHOULD 1; mutation-verified, 2026-10-05: astra r1's exact repro, rejecting bare-plus-a-second-argument, fails only that one of six, and four further targeted mutations each fail exactly their own combination), `packages/nana-pack/tests/code-map.test.mjs::new URL(...) resolves to an edge with ${f.name}` (astra r2 MUST 2, astra r3 MUST 3: ten distinct-target fixtures, one per promised whitespace position — between import and (, between new and URL, between URL and (, inside URL(...)'s parens around the literal and the comma, around each dot of import.meta.url, before AND after the suffix dot for both .href and .pathname, and a newline between the URL's arguments and .href; mutation-verified, 2026-10-05, one targeted regex mutation per position: each of the ten fails exactly the fixture(s) sharing that token — the one shared token, "after the comma, before import.meta.url", fails all ten, since every fixture carries a plain space there; no mutation fails a fixture it should not), `packages/nana-pack/tests/code-map.test.mjs::new URL(...) resolves to an edge from inside a generator method's yielded import (astra r3)`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/code-map.test.mjs::a template-string URL, a variable, and a non-relative literal all resolve to nothing`; the template's own fixture-level tests (templates/typescript/template/tests/code-map.test.ts.jinja, the same six-combination loop, the same ten whitespace forms, the generator-method case, and the exclusions test) manually verified green against a fresh render (vitest 80/80, 2026-10-05) |
| R-861 | WHEN --impact runs, the TypeScript code-map generator shall print the count of test modules (modules under a layerExempt/testRoots root) that import no mapped module. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/code-map.test.mjs::--impact's output carries the untraced-test line with the real count`, `packages/nana-pack/tests/templates-render.test.mjs::${language}: --impact prints the untraced-test count for a fresh scaffold`; the template's own fixture-level test (templates/typescript/template/tests/code-map.test.ts.jinja, "--impact also reports how many test-root modules import no mapped module at all") manually verified green against a fresh render, same as R-860 |
| R-862 | WHEN --impact runs, the Python code-map generator shall print the count of test modules (modules under a layerExempt/testRoots root) that import no mapped module. | implemented | `packages/nana-pack/tests/templates-render.test.mjs::${language}: --impact prints the untraced-test count for a fresh scaffold` — the same marked test, looped over both languages; python3 was installed on the machine this lane ran on, so this clause ran for real rather than SKIPping. The template's own fixture-level test (templates/python/template/tests/test_code_map.py.jinja, test_impact_also_reports_untraced_test_modules) manually verified green against a fresh render with uv run pytest, 2026-10-05 |
| R-863 | The TypeScript code-map generator shall accept a match of any of its four import-detection patterns only when the from/import keyword it starts with sits in executable code, never inside a line comment, a block comment, a quoted string, or template-literal text. | retired | retired 2026-10-05 (map-test-links lane, astra r2 round 3, `docs/reviews/map-test-links-2026-10-05/astra-r2.md`): the lexer this row named fixed the class it targeted (comment/string text making a spurious edge — 0 edges changed on nana-pi either way) while itself LOSING real edges in ordinary code (a division misread right after a completed template or object literal; a `//` comment ended by CR or U+2028, not just LF) and still fabricating edges from valid multi-line strings (an escaped CRLF continuation, a raw U+2028, both legal inside a string). A lost edge is worse than the spurious edge it replaced. Subtracted before landing; superseded by R-864's guard, also later retired. Id never reused. |
| R-864 | WHEN a module names an import whose keyword (from/import) sits on a line whose first non-blank characters are //, or are * with no */ anywhere on that line, the TypeScript code-map generator shall record no import edge for it. | retired | retired 2026-10-05 (map-test-links lane, astra r3, `docs/reviews/map-test-links-2026-10-05/astra-r3.md`): the guard this row named lost real imports — a generator method (`*load() { yield import(...) }`), a leading `*` multiplying an awaited import's property, and a `//` that opens a line only because a multi-line string or a backslash continuation put it there. Each is ordinary, valid JavaScript. Subtracted before landing; the four patterns match raw source again, as on `main`, with no filter in its place (see Open questions). Id never reused. |

Amendment 1 §A3 overrules the earlier config-path wording: the Words bullet now names the banned word in a code span (Jake banned, then the word itself), since a pi session has no memory of a ban and will not open a file the rule merely points at. "A decision point" is now a numbered list (Amendment 1 §A2), ending the 27-word sentence astra r1 found escaping the cap through soft-wrap. Wording per Amendment 1 §A2 and §A3, ratified in land-ruling.md §2 |

## 33. The four buffer caps

| ID | Requirement | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-429 | WHEN a child's stdout line passes the line cap, whether it arrives in pieces or whole with its newline, the desk shall discard that line, keep the session running, tell that session's clients once, and reject every RPC in flight on that child. | implemented | `apps/desk/test/buffer-caps.test.mjs::…the RPC whose response was discarded is rejected, not left on its timer`, `apps/desk/test/buffer-caps.test.mjs::…clients are TOLD, once, that a line was dropped`, `apps/desk/test/buffer-caps.test.mjs::…a terminated line over the cap is dropped too, not parsed and broadcast`, `apps/desk/test/buffer-caps.test.mjs::…and the child is still running (a bad line does not cost the session)` |
| R-430 | WHEN one event-stream client's retained output passes its cap, the desk shall end that response and drop its socket rather than throttle the fan-out, leaving the other clients on that session uninterrupted. | implemented | `apps/desk/test/buffer-caps.test.mjs::a client that stops reading does not stall the flood for everyone`, `apps/desk/test/buffer-caps.test.mjs::…the desk disconnects it once its write buffer passes the cap`, `apps/desk/test/buffer-caps.test.mjs::…and its response is really ended, not left open and buffering`, `apps/desk/test/buffer-caps.test.mjs::…the healthy client on the same session kept every event` |
| R-431 | WHEN a session already holds the maximum in-flight RPCs, a further request shall fail fast with 429 without disturbing those in flight, the cap bounding concurrency rather than rate. | implemented | `apps/desk/test/buffer-caps.test.mjs::the ${PENDING_CAP + 1}th concurrent RPC fails fast with 429`, `apps/desk/test/buffer-caps.test.mjs::…the first ${PENDING_CAP} are untouched and still answer on their own timers`, `apps/desk/test/buffer-caps.test.mjs::…and the cap is on IN-FLIGHT work only: the next RPC after they drain is fine` |
| R-432 | WHEN an app data command's stdout passes its cap, the desk shall kill the process on size well before the time limit, reap it, and answer 500 naming the cap. | implemented | `apps/desk/test/buffer-caps.test.mjs::a data command past the output cap → 500 naming the cap`, `apps/desk/test/buffer-caps.test.mjs::…and it is killed on SIZE, well before the 20 s timeout`, `apps/desk/test/buffer-caps.test.mjs::…the process is reaped, not left flooding a dead pipe` |
| R-433 | The desk shall bound the per-child status, widget and unanswered-dialog maps a child fills through its own events. | violated | unbounded for the life of the session in handleChildEvent, apps/desk/server.mjs, and replayed whole in every hello; declared in README Known limits and STILL OPEN in docs/review-punchlist-2026-09-08.md |

| R-489 | Every async continuation that touches the transcript, the header, the editor or the live handle shall return without painting anything, toasts included, when the stage generation it began in is stale. | untested | split from R-434 2026-10-04 (EARS form batch C): no recorded red mutation per named condition; 6b worklist |

## 35. Stage signing keys and block provenance

| ID | Requirement | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-437 | The desk shall record the keys it issues as one file per session named by the pi session header id, mode 0600 inside a 0700 directory, written temp-file-then-rename, holding at most 8 keys most recent first, refusing a session id that is not name-shaped, and retrying a failed save on the next record for that session. | implemented | `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::store: one v1 record per session, named by the pi session header id`, `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::store: record mode 0600`, `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::store: directory mode 0700`, `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::store: capped at 8 keys, most recent first`, `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::store: a path-shaped session id is refused, not written`, `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::store: ...and leaves the previous record byte-identical`, `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::store: ...and a LATER record of the SAME key retries the save` |
| R-438 | WHEN an app session the desk has a record for is resumed, including under a renamed file, it shall be handed that session's most recent recorded key so blocks minted before the restart still verify. | implemented | `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::run 2: the resumed child was handed the SAME key the store recorded for that session`, `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::run 2: a block minted before the restart is STILL unredacted`, `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::run 3: renamed session file resumes and its old block still verifies (keyed by header id, not path)` |
| R-439 | The ledger read shall verify each block against the live child's key plus every key recorded for the session the child reports holding. | implemented | `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::run 4: every block of session B verifies, across BOTH keys the desk issued for it`, `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::run 2: a hand-forged signature is still redacted`, `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::run 2: a block signed under a key this desk never issued is still redacted` |
| R-491 | The ledger read shall redact a forged signature or a key this desk never issued. | untested | split from R-439 2026-10-04 (EARS form batch C): no recorded red mutation per named condition; 6b worklist |
| R-440 | The live tool-result path shall accept only a block signed by this child's own key, so a recorded but foreign key of the same session does not pass there. | implemented | `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::run 3: LIVE path unchanged — a block signed with a recorded but FOREIGN key is dropped`, `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::run 3: LIVE path still passes this child's own block` |
| R-441 | WHEN a fork or clone runs, the new session's record shall be seeded from the source the child reported before the fork. | implemented | `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::run 3: the fork's record was seeded from the source, not just given this child's key`, `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::run 3: an unconfirmed fork inherits NOTHING; its copied blocks stay redacted`, `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::run 3: a confirmation response delayed 1000 ms after request receipt confirms nothing` |
| R-492 | IF a fork or clone runs and the destination is not confirmed inside the budget, an answer landing on or after the deadline included, THEN the fork shall inherit nothing. | untested | split from R-441 2026-10-04 (EARS form batch C): no recorded red mutation per named condition; 6b worklist |
| R-442 | Session-changing commands shall run one at a time per app child with queued changes bounded, answering 429 past the bound and freeing slots on every outcome. | implemented | `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::run 3: the lifecycle queue is bounded — past the cap a session change is refused at once with 429`, `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::run 3: while a transition is held, no queued transition sends anything to the child`, `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::run 3: the queue's slots are released — a later lifecycle RPC still works` |
| R-443 | IF the child's current session cannot be established, THEN no recorded-key widening shall apply at all. | implemented | `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::run 3: an unresolved get_state drops the recorded-key widening entirely (no stale authority)`, `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::run 3: ...and it verifies again as soon as the session can be established` |
| R-444 | The hygiene pass shall delete only records issued under this desk's own sessions root, only when that enumeration succeeded and came back non-empty, and never a file it could not itself have written. | implemented | `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::store: an EMPTY session enumeration prunes nothing`, `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::two roots: ...and keeps the default-root desk's records (live AND not in its enumeration)`, `apps/desk/test/stage-key-persistence.test.mjs::store: the prune leaves a file it could not have written (a dotted name is not a session id)` |
| R-445 | Two desks writing one session's record shall each keep what the other added. | violated | no cross-process lock by design, so simultaneous writers keep only the last writer's additions and the store holds no copy of what was lost; declared in README Known limits and the punchlist open sub-items |
| R-446 | The stage-key store shall not block the desk's event loop while it reads or writes. | violated | its per-session reads and writes and the one-time enumeration are synchronous on the event loop, so a hung filesystem stalls the whole process; declared in README Known limits |

## 41. Measurement and accounting

| ID | Requirement | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-503 | Token arithmetic shall carry pi's own field names and pi's own totals rather than recomputing them, a run's own spend being its assistant messages plus pi's cache-warm usage entries counted as entries. | implemented | `apps/bench/test/usage.test.mjs::usage uses pi-ai's field names, not ours`, `apps/bench/test/usage.test.mjs::tokens.input sums the ASSISTANT messages`, `apps/bench/test/usage.test.mjs::own total is pi's totalTokens, summed`, `apps/bench/test/usage.test.mjs::a pi cache-warm usage entry is own spend (tokens)`, `apps/bench/test/usage.test.mjs::…counted as a usage ENTRY, not an assistant message` |
| R-504 | Nested model spend shall be kept strictly apart from own spend so the reported spend adds each exactly once. | implemented | `apps/bench/test/usage.test.mjs::own tokens EXCLUDE the nested call (no double counting)`, `apps/bench/test/nested.test.mjs::own + nested = the true total (astra's 1,530 + 4,810 = 6,340)`, `apps/bench/test/nested.test.mjs::a request outside any tool window is NOT counted`, `apps/bench/test/nested.test.mjs::a request AFTER the window closes is not counted`, `apps/bench/test/nested.test.mjs::an unwatched tool does not open a counting window`, `apps/bench/test/nested.test.mjs::…and is recorded as a skipped own call` |
| R-546 | Nested model spend shall be counted only while a watched tool is executing. | untested | split from R-504 2026-10-04 (EARS form batch C): no recorded red mutation per named condition; 6b worklist |
| R-547 | A model request outside any watched-tool execution window shall be recorded as a skipped own call so the scoping is auditable. | untested | split from R-504 2026-10-04 (EARS form batch C): no recorded red mutation per named condition; 6b worklist |
| R-505 | A watched tool with no observed network, an unfinished, failed or unparseable harvest, a model connection on a transport the wrapper cannot read, and a phase the tool reports but nothing measured shall each mark the window unknown with a reason rather than zero, inventing no usage. | implemented | `apps/bench/test/nested.test.mjs::a watched tool that produced NO observable network is unknown, not free`, `apps/bench/test/nested.test.mjs::…with a reason a reviewer can act on`, `apps/bench/test/nested.test.mjs::…and no usage invented for it`, `apps/bench/test/nested.test.mjs::an unfinished body read is reported as unknown, not as no-spend`, `apps/bench/test/nested.test.mjs::a failed request (network error) marks unknown`, `apps/bench/test/nested.test.mjs::an unparseable body marks unknown rather than 0`, `apps/bench/test/nested.test.mjs::a WebSocket model connection inside a window marks it unknown`, `apps/bench/test/nested.test.mjs::a model phase we never measured is detected from the tool's own report`, `apps/bench/test/nested.test.mjs::…AND the window is unknown, despite having measured traffic` |
| R-506 | Money shall come from pi's own cost function with nested spend bucketed and priced per model. | implemented | `apps/bench/test/usage.test.mjs::real stream: cost is summed from pi's own per-message cost`, `apps/bench/test/nested.test.mjs::each bucket is priced with ITS OWN model, not the first one seen`, `apps/bench/test/usage.test.mjs::nested spend with no pricer is null, not 0`, `apps/bench/test/usage.test.mjs::…and says why`, `apps/bench/test/usage.test.mjs::unknown nested spend: the reason names the unmeasured part`, `apps/bench/test/usage.test.mjs::unknown nested spend: the observed bound is STILL the known dollars, not 0` |
| R-548 | Money shall be null with a reason whenever any part went unmeasured or unpriced. | untested | split from R-506 2026-10-04 (EARS form batch C): no recorded red mutation per named condition; 6b worklist |
| R-549 | Money shall keep the priced part separately as an explicit lower bound. | untested | split from R-506 2026-10-04 (EARS form batch C): no recorded red mutation per named condition; 6b worklist |
| R-507 | A run shall be recorded as decided successful only on the terminal settled marker AND no tool call left without a result AND a passing checker. | implemented | `apps/bench/test/usage.test.mjs::complete when settled, no errors, nothing dangling`, `apps/bench/test/usage.test.mjs::truncated: agent_end present but NOT settled`, `apps/bench/test/usage.test.mjs::truncated: dangling tool call detected`, `apps/bench/test/usage.test.mjs::a stream with a plausible answer but no terminator cannot pass`, `apps/bench/test/integration.test.mjs::executeRun: a passing run is state ok`, `apps/bench/test/integration.test.mjs::executeRun: the checker verdict is carried` |

# Part G. General engineering requirements (every nana project — nana-pi itself included)

These rows are the same in every nana project and are **never renumbered or edited
per project** — they arrive with the template and are re-synced by `uvx copier update`.
What changes per project is the Status and Evidence column: promote a row when a test
in this repo pins it, and say where.

Where a row says *every module*, that is every module under the roots declared in
`code-map.config.json`: 172 modules in all (`npm run map:check`'s own count) — 77 non-test
(the four packages' `lib/`, `bin/` and `extensions/`, both apps and this repo's own
`scripts/`) plus 95 under the six test roots, each declared `layerExempt` so a test may
import across the layer direction — not declared outside the map. The recorded study trees
and `templates/` (whose headers name the RENDERED project's paths) are declared outside the
map by `ignore`; three bench modules content-pinned by a closed study are
declared `exempt` with their reasons. Generated and checked by `npm run map` /
`npm run map:check` — shims over the generator the templates ship.

## Open questions

- (pi 1.0 lane, 2026-10-04) mcp.json's own shape failures (not JSON, not an object, mcpServers not an object) are exercised by three uncited checks in packages/nana-setup/tests/doctor-detail.test.mjs but have no row. Add an R-366-shaped row, or declare them R-365's error handling.
- (writing trial, astra r3, 2026-10-04) R-753/R-755's cap is a CHARACTER budget on the injected block, while the read ceiling (R-755) is a BYTE budget on the file. A valid multi-byte-heavy rule (e.g. a 2,000-character Chinese file) can be cut well short of 4,000 characters while the notice still reads "cut at 4000 chars" — the notice is imprecise in that direction. Document the distinction; the byte/character gap is accepted, not fixed, for the trial.

1. The pack's README is 721 lines of declared behaviour and produced 258 rows; the extraction notes name a safe trim (merge same-status rows inside the gate, handoff and pi-review sections). Trim, or keep the grain?
2. R-058 (closed by t1-gate): pi code-loading configuration in active and default agent dirs is on the policy floor.
3. The repo test runner's 17 rows are pinned only by its own `--self-test`; an external test is the honest closure.
4. (closed 2026-10-02) The stale doc claims the extraction found are corrected, and the README check (G-012) now runs over all seven READMEs in `readme-check.config.json`.
5. (closed 2026-10-02) `packages/nana-stage/README.md` exists and is in the checked set.
6b. **Clause-level honesty.** The form half closed 2026-10-04 (`docs/reviews/ears-form-2026-10-04/lane-close-ruling.md`); the coverage half is this question. Origin: sol r3 2026-10-02 showed the rail proves marker identity and citation existence, not clause coverage; a 25-row sample pinned fully in 16 (64%). The EARS-form lane then split every multi-`shall` row: 200 origins into 244 new rows, 0 off form, G-013 `implemented`. `EARS_ALLOWANCE` sits at 0, sealed and held equal to the measured count (R-757). The lane judged the rows it touched under a standard that tightened as it ran. A3 and B's `implemented` rows (10 and 10 split-born, 9 and 7 origins) carry one recorded red mutation per named condition. In those, astra read every record and replayed one in five (`bc-method-ruling.md`). A1 and A2's (32 and 30 split-born, 33 and 28 origins) carry one red mutation per row, sampled 20 per batch at 19 of 20. C's 44 origins and the 289 implemented rows never off form carry their pre-lane cites, unjudged. Under the per-condition standard the worker-claimed rows had an uncovered condition in 27 of 46 (A3) and 66 of 83 (B). Each now reads `untested` with the uncovered words in its cell. The lane left 214 rows `untested`: 161 split-born and 53 downgraded origins. By batch: 7, 5, 9, 34, 90 and 69 for batch 0, A1, A2, A3, B and C. It ran no closing sample (`lane-close-ruling.md` §3). Worklist, in order. (1) The 214 untested rows, read from their cells: A3 and B's quote the uncovered words, the rest carry a batch sentence. (2) The re-judge tier: A1 and A2's 62 implemented split-born rows and 61 origins, then C's 44 origins, then the 289. Method is open. Per-condition records did not converge from the worker side (58.7% then 79.5% gap); the reviewer's text read found every gap the mutation hunt found. The next design starts there. Until a cell names a per-condition record, an implemented row means: one mutation turns one cited check red; read the cell.
6. The desk's page layer (stage-generation counter, resync door, bash card caps, /reload, activity line, dashboards' ports) is covered only by browser suites `npm test` never runs: untested by the suite of record.
7. (ears-form batch 0, astra r1 NOTE, 2026-10-04; RULED by astra r2, 2026-10-04) R-757 is a DEVIATION: design-ruling.md §1 only requires the rail to fail when the off-form count *exceeds* the allowance (a ceiling), so a count that quietly drops below the allowance — stale headroom — was never caught; the allowance only ever tightens by a human editing it at landing. R-757 adds a nana-pi-only assertion that the measured count EQUALS `EARS_ALLOWANCE`, failing loudly on any drift either direction. A fresh scaffold needs no equivalent row, because both its measured count and its default allowance are 0. **Ruling (astra r2): keep R-757 as a nana-pi-only repository-suite invariant.** It supplements the ceiling — it does not auto-lower the allowance, and it does not stop one old off-form row being exchanged for a new one. Generic `check()` and both templates' behaviour stay the ceiling-only `count > allowance`, unchanged; the enforcement is additive and lives only in `packages/nana-pack/tests/requirements-trace.test.mjs`, never in the reusable rail. Closed at landing (Fable, batch0-land-ruling.md §2): kept. Any commit that moves the off-form count moves `EARS_ALLOWANCE` and its seal in the same commit.
8. (ears-form batch 0, astra r2 RESIDUALS to record, 2026-10-04) (a) The form check (G-013) counts `shall` tokens; it does not establish that a row makes one semantic promise, nor that its clause has test coverage — form compliance is orthogonal to the clause-level honesty residual already tracked at 6b. (b) Generic `check()`/template allowances remain ceilings only (`count > allowance`); R-757's equality enforcement is a nana-pi-only suite addition, not a change to the reusable rail's or either template's semantics. (c) `packages/nana-pack/tests/templates-render.test.mjs` does not execute the rendered language suites itself; a separate manual acceptance run (`uvx copier copy` + `pnpm install && pnpm test` / `uv sync && uv run pytest`) stays a required step this lane did by hand each round, not something the automated suite can yet claim. (d) This worktree's missing root `node_modules` and `apps/bench/.ext` mean a green full-repository `npm test` cannot be claimed while those are absent — only the EARS-touched tests are claimed green here; `packages/nana-pack/tests/readme-check.test.mjs`'s 5 environmental problems are pre-existing and unrelated to this lane.
9. (ears-form batch 0, Fable) About 150 three-digit IDs remain after the lane. Widening to four digits is a rail change in both templates and in aml-desk's copy; the seat's call. The rail's older behaviours (markers, cites, statuses) have no rows of their own; only the form check does.
10. (map-test-links lane, 2026-10-05) R-860 only teaches the TypeScript code-map generator the `new URL(<literal>, import.meta.url)` dynamic-import form. A dynamic import whose argument is NOT a string literal — built from a template string, a variable, or any other expression — still resolves to no edge, SILENTLY: the generator records no problem for it, unlike the Python generator, which fails `--check` naming it `unmapped dynamic import at line N`. Measured on nana-pi (2026-10-05): about 56 such sites exist in this repo alone (pi's own entry point, a handful of temp-file loaders, code built inside a spawned child-process script's own source string) — making the TypeScript generator fail on them the way Python's does would turn every TypeScript project (nana-pi included) red on day one. Not changed in this lane. Jake's call later: fail the same way Python does (and fix the ~56 sites), or document the asymmetry and leave it. In a `.ts` project a bare `import(new URL(…))` is a type error, so users tend to write `String(new URL(…))`, `new URL(…).toString()` or `fileURLToPath(new URL(…))` instead; all three are in this silent class (land ruling, 2026-10-05).
11. (map-test-links lane, 2026-10-05) A separate, pre-existing gap found while measuring R-862 on a fresh Python scaffold: the Python generator's import graph does not resolve a bare `import code_map` (or `import readme_check`, `import requirements_trace`) the way `tests/test_code_map.py`, `tests/test_readme_check.py` and `tests/test_requirements_trace.py` actually import the script they exercise (via a `sys.path` insert, not a dotted `scripts.code_map` import) — so those three test modules, plus `tests/conftest.py`, show zero callees in a fresh scaffold (4 of 5 test modules `untraced tests` reports there, against 1 of 5 for the equivalent TypeScript scaffold). This is unrelated to dynamic imports (item 10) and to R-860/861/862, which only claim the PRINTED count is correct, not that the underlying graph is complete — not measured or fixed in this lane. Jake's call later: teach `dotted_name`/`_link_imports` to also recognize a bare top-level name that matches a mapped script's own basename, or leave it as a known Python-generator blind spot.
12. (map-test-links lane, 2026-10-05; rewritten round 4 after the SECOND filter tried here
    was also subtracted — astra r3, `docs/reviews/map-test-links-2026-10-05/astra-r3.md`)
    The four import-detection patterns (STATIC_FROM, BARE_IMPORT, DYNAMIC, DYNAMIC_URL)
    match raw source text, exactly as on `main` before this lane, with no filter standing
    between a match and an edge. Raw matching can create a SPURIOUS edge from text that
    merely looks like an import but is not executable: inside a single- or double-quoted
    string, inside a template literal, or inside a `//` or `/* ... */` comment — for all
    four patterns. A spurious edge is not a safe direction to fail in: it can SILENTLY
    suppress the untraced-test count `--impact` prints (a real gap in coverage now reads as
    covered), or it can make `--check` fail, loudly, naming a module that does not exist.
    Two filters were tried here, in order, and both subtracted before landing, because each
    one LOST a real import instead — a strictly worse failure than the spurious edge either
    was built to remove: R-863 (astra r1's comment/string-aware lexer, retired by astra r2)
    and R-864 (astra r2's narrower comment-line guard, retired by astra r3). Neither
    replacement is attempted a third time in this lane; distinguishing real code from a
    spurious-looking specifier without a real parser kept costing more than it bought. The
    Python generator carries no equivalent class of gap: its reader walks a real AST, so a
    comment or a string is never ambiguous with code by construction, not by a pattern the
    TypeScript side has to guess at. Jake's call later: build a real parser (a materially
    bigger lift, and a new dependency this generator is deliberately without today), or
    accept the gap as documented.
    Remedy today, when a spurious edge fails `--check` naming a module that does not exist:
    add that path to the `ignore` list in `code-map.config.json`, as nana-pi already does
    for three paths that exist only as text inside a test (land ruling, 2026-10-05).

## Deliberate omissions

- Study data under `apps/bench/studies/**` is evidence, never a requirement.
- The pack gate is advisory by doctrine (a load-path convenience, not a security boundary); no row claims otherwise.
- Nothing in the catch ledger E1 may change roles, models or spend (HANDOFF); rows record the posture, not a licence.
